What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've been struggling a lot with focusing and staying on task lately. Was there any major stress or mood change around the time they began?\n\nPatient: These issues actually started back in childhood, definitely before I was 12, but they've been consistently really bad for about a year and 11 months now. I don't recall any major life stressors or mood changes happening around the time they started.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying excessively about many different things, and when that happens, is it the worry itself that makes it hard to concentrate?\n\nPatient: I don't really experience that kind of constant worrying. I don't think anxiety is the cause of my concentration difficulties, as my mind just seems to drift off even when I'm completely calm.\n\nDoctor: Besides trouble focusing, do you also often lose things, forget appointments or tasks, and have trouble organizing, and do these problems happen in more than one setting like work and home?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I don't really experience losing my things, forgetting appointments, or having trouble keeping things organized. However, I don't really struggle with making careless mistakes, avoiding tasks, or not listening when people are talking to me.\n\nDoctor: Have these attention problems caused clear difficulties in at least two areas of your life, such as work, home, school, or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, they definitely cause clear difficulties in multiple areas of my life. I really struggle to finish chores at home and often fail to complete my work duties because I just can't seem to follow through on instructions and finish what I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks, and do you often seem not to listen when spoken to directly?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely struggle with following through on instructions and often fail to finish tasks or chores that I start. As for appearing not to listen when spoken to directly, I'm not as sure about that and don't think it's really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: When a task takes a long time or a lot of mental effort, do you often avoid it or put it off?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I avoid starting tasks that require a lot of effort. My main issue is that once I do start them, I really struggle to stay focused on them or actually finish them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes or miss details on work or other tasks even when you understand what to do?\n\nPatient: I don't really struggle with making careless mistakes or missing details on my tasks. That hasn't really been a major issue for me."

Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event. Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future. Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
